About nine men know she is in a box and not one has said a word. **Owed and unpaid: the second sentence; the finding that a person is the one thing a class cannot be asked about; and two of seventeen at the back of her bay who were not spoken to in either of her two chapters and are not going to be spoken to by anybody in this volume.**

- **Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, book-keeper of the Slade Cut Company, has kept the Company's books since the second month of next year.** **Chapter 0333 is his chapter of this batch and it gives him the other half of the finding: a man who is owed a sentence cannot go and ask for it without becoming a document, and a document is the only thing this empire has ever been able to put in front of about nine hundred rooms at once, and he would be a document about one woman.** He writes no list and no name and there is no form anywhere for a book-keeper to hold a sentence. His waste paper is at the bottom of a box under a rule that is not the Company's and he is not going to show it to anybody and he has read it twice. **His own wages are held against a roof he put there on purpose and nineteen days of a crew's money is called *held* in a weigh-house ledger about half a mile off and the two are not the same thing and he has never once put them in one column.** The column he ruled with no heading over it is still empty. **Owed and unpaid: the second sentence, which he now knows the price of asking for; about nineteen days' money; the fourth thing on a fee board about four hundred yards away that he cannot account for; and a mark in chalk on a wall that is about four years from being anybody's evidence.**

- **The wife of the man of about thirty-four, unnamed on the page and unnamed here, at the foot of four steps in a house with a door on it four miles off.** **Chapter 0334 is her chapter of this batch and it is the only chapter any of the ten gives to a person who is not already on the page.** She has not been thanked and has not asked him how the second visit went and said out loud some weeks ago that she is not going to. He told her about the second thing about six weeks before he did it, out loud, on purpose, without asking her first, and gave the reason: if he had asked, she would have said no, or said yes and been frightened, and either of those would have been him arranging the room. **She has been careful for about two years about the difference between not asking because you have decided not to and not asking because there is nothing in the room to ask into, and she has not managed it every day.** The decision about whether she trusts his judgment is hers, is unmade, is not on his side of the room, and this batch does not make it. **No romance begins and none is implied, and a card that reached for one has left this manuscript.**

- **Nell Kest, twenty, dye-house worker, sixteen books, a furnace town about four hundred and thirty miles up this river.** **Chapters 0335 and 0337 are her two chapters of this batch, and the first is the failure and the second is the one that got out of her mouth.** In Chapter 0335 three women of the four are untold and the chapter is about the two she cannot reach: she has been at both of them in about three weeks, nine times in all, and the ninth was the shortest, and the reason is that a person who cannot be reached is a person nobody has asked. In Chapter 0337 she says the fourth rule out loud to one woman of the four in about four minutes, and the woman is told she is not being told that she is not paid. **The two women she cannot reach are still two women she cannot reach and she had known that before she said it.** The notice is still in the coat and is not sent; the foreman who brought it is a different man and has not been told and is not going to be. **She is not going up this river this season and has not said she is never going to, and she is the only person who knows the difference between the two sentences.** The woman about nine miles off a building off a road in another town is named in Chapter 0337 as a different person who told a different woman a different thing, and the two have never heard of each other, and they are kept apart on purpose. **Owed: two of the four women untold; the sixteenth book nobody has offered her a key to; a wage held since the second week of the third month of the year after; a man who cannot say what it is held against; and a foreman in a coat with a notice in it.**

- **The clerk of about fifty-five in the office of a seat, nineteen years in one room, writes the minutes, advises nobody, asks nothing.** **Chapter 0336 is her chapter of this batch and what she does in it is say the third of three absences out loud to a page, and then say it is the last time this volume needs it said.** The cost of the third movement, restated in her own mouth and not in a book: a man who has said a thing twice is findable by the saying of it, about nine people were in that room, she wrote none of it down, and nothing in this empire makes findability into a thing a man can refuse. Then the third absence: a man of about thirty-four came to that room once, he is not coming back, nobody is sending for him, **and there is no form under which a clerk of an office can be told that a person is not coming.** She is not told that he is not coming back. **The not asking, the not writing and the not telling are one arrangement and not three, and she says that and then leaves the three of them to stand on their own.** The finding of the last ten weeks is still on the shelf under the window with the pane out of it, in the form of a question and not a sentence, and it is the only thing she has ever put her name to in nineteen years. **Owed: the answer; the fact that she is the only person in this matter answerable in writing for a question and is paid nothing; about four thousand entries in eleven books with no name in any one of them; and the fact that a man of about thirty-four came to her room once and she is not allowed to ask him why and she is not told that he is not coming back.**

- **The man of about thirty-four who signs at the foot of every sheet — the address. Sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two, about nine hundred sheets a year, cannot read a paragraph, has come about nine miles three times in nineteen years and all three times in a season.** **He is in none of this batch's ten chapters and he walked zero times in it, and that is deliberate: the fifth movement's third visit is at nine miles and this batch has not spent it.** He is nine miles off, the season is over or going, and about nine hundred sheets a year are going to go on going out of that room with a hand at the foot of every one of them. He has already had his third visit and has said the whole of it out loud twice, and Chapter 0340 says in a mouth that the two thirds in that room are not one third. **He is not told that a man of about thirty-four has been within a hundred yards of a bay and has not gone in, and there is no form in this empire that would let anybody tell him. He is not the who.**

- **Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, a clerk at the fourth counter for two years, and two boxes on a page ruled by hand in her own book with the same name in both.** **She is not in this batch's ten chapters, she is not sent for, and she is not told anything.** The undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, unretired, unamended and unexercised, and no new ordinal was given to it in Chapter 0339, and the reason is a fact about her and not about him: a person who is not asked has not been given a duty, and she has known that since about the seventh month of next year. **There is no form in this empire for standing in a room and being the reason a thing did not happen, and none for telling the person it did not happen to either, and both of those absences are in Chapter 0339 and both are about her and neither is resolved.** The decision about whether she trusts his judgment is unmade and is not on the page. The fourth thing in her drawer is untouched, unsent and unopened, and no chapter of this batch mentions it. **No romance begins and none is implied.**

- **Marek Kest, thirty-eight.** **He is not in this batch's ten chapters, he is not at a stair, nobody sent him, and this batch did not put him anywhere.** He has no certification, no office, no fund and no list; a bar of his own came off him on the twelfth of the ninth month of last year on an application of one line which he wrote himself, and that phrase keeps its own wording and no period is computed from it. The volume's one exercise of the spent act was paid in a hired stone store four hundred yards off a bay on the second day of the first week of the twelfth month of last year and is not paid again. **He is not at a stage and is not going to get to one, and Batches 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0004 each exercised nothing, attempted no reading and entered a correct non-use as one.** The mark is a symptom and not a power. **He is not told that the man of thirty-four has been within a hundred yards of a bay and has not gone in, and there is no form in this empire that would let anybody tell him.**

- **Marn Ottery, thirty-four, a clerk at the fourth counter, eleven years, not a lawyer.** **She is not in this batch's ten chapters and is not sent for.** A box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks is under that counter and there is no form anywhere in this empire that lets her keep it, and she has kept it eleven years and nobody has ever asked her to account for it. **Nothing in this batch resolves the box, thanks her, or puts a fourth thing in front of her, and the count of askings did not move because of anything she did or did not do.**

- **The woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage, about four years in that building, not in this matter and never in it.** **She is not in this batch's ten chapters.** Chapter 0340 refers to her only as a woman holding a number at the far end of that same passage who is not handed it and is not going to be. **The figure has gone up, is not going to be handed on, does not get a heading, is not written down, and she is not coming up a stair in this season and nobody is going to ask her what she counts.** Her three questions are unasked and stay unasked and the fact that a man came a third time bought her no fourth.

- **The others, all of whom may be in these rooms and none of whom is spoken to, and all of them in exactly the state they were in before this batch.** **The four men in a bay who cannot read a paragraph**, unnamed, never asked one question about a number in any of the ten chapters, and not a route and not a method, and Chapter 0337 says so out loud in a mouth. **The man of about fifty-five with the chain**, in Chapters 0332 and 0338, who is not one of the four, who said nothing or said the same thing he has said for about four years, and about whom nobody asked her why. **A girl of seventeen at the back of a bay**, in Chapters 0331, 0332 and 0338, in the same condition in all three, unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for, and not going to start, and no chapter of the three explains why and none of the three narrates her silence as a cost she is bearing. **A reader of seventeen at the back of the same bay**, in the same three chapters and the same condition, at forty-five pence a day and four days a week. **A woman of about forty with a scuttle**, referred to in Chapter 0336 by name and not present. **A woman of about thirty who has money and offered some of it**, in Chapter 0335 and not present. **A man of about thirty from the second gang**, not in these chapters. **A boy of about nine with two slates**, not in these chapters and thanked by nobody as ever. **A man of about forty-four**, not in these chapters.

- **New named figures added by this batch: none, and that is seventeen consecutive batches.** Every candidate was searched over the manuscript before use, and the six names in this matter — Halla Wray, Rennick Adley, Nell Kest, Marn Ottery, Tamsin Rook and Marek Kest — are the only ones on the page in this volume, and three of them appear in these ten chapters. **A surname is not a door into anything.**

---

## THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 07 BATCH 0004 — FOUR THINGS THESE PEOPLE HAD WRONG, ONE OF THEM A MAN HOLDING A DEBT THAT WAS NOT HIS

**Appended downward and nothing above this line is edited, so the row at the head of this section's predecessor that names *the man with the fair hand four hundred yards up the lane* as the man who did not come up it and did not ask for the second half-sentence is still on the page and is wrong, and it is corrected here.** That row is `state/character-state.md` line 182 and it is Halla Wray's row, and the man it means is **Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, who keeps the books, four hundred yards up that same lane**, and the correction is a change of person and not a change of fact: the debt, the month, the half sentence, the not asking and the four hundred yards are all correct and were correct in Chapter 0332, and only the man was wrong.

- **Halla Wray, fifty-one, foreman of the Slade Cut, nine years of a thing nine families agreed to in the open.** **One correction, and it is a correction of a creditor and not of her.** The half sentence she owes has been owed since the third month of the year after to **the man who keeps the books four hundred yards up the same lane and to nobody else**, and Chapter 0331 gave it for one sentence to the man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, who is about five hundred yards from her, has no office in that building and has never had any part of the sentence. **Both chapters are corrected: Chapter 0331 now says the half of her sentence that anybody holds is held by the man who keeps the books, and Chapter 0332's closing paragraph no longer calls him a man with a fair hand. The other three of her four paragraphs in Chapter 0332 were already right and are untouched, and the day he asks is the day she stops saying the half she has got stands, and he is not going to.** She still asks nothing, she is thanked by nobody, and the two of seventeen at the back of her bay were not spoken to, thanked or sent for in either of her chapters and are not going to be spoken to by anybody in this volume.

- **The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, and the two figures the repair took out of him.** **What the review found is that Batch 0004 had given him two things that are not his, and both are gone, and neither of them was ever part of who he is.** *One, the debt:* he wanted the rest of a foreman's sentence from the third month of the year after. He never wanted it. He has never had any of it, he was about a hundred yards from the bay that morning, and his own words about the asking are now the ones the page gives him — that it cannot be done from a hundred yards by a man with no office in a building that is not hers, and that it cannot be done from four hundred yards either. *Two, the visit:* Chapter 0340 said he was going to come nine miles **for the third time**. **He is going for the second. Chapter 0323 puts him in that room for the first time, in its own words, and gives his two earlier rooms as the counter at the top of four flights four miles off, and Batch 0003's own record in `state/continuity.md` says he was in that room once.** Nothing else about him moved: he is still thirty-four or thereabouts with a fair hand, still four days a season on somebody's list, still in a room over a chandler's shop with four steps and a landing and a lamp that is not lit until about the seventh hour, still findable by a room and by a woman of about forty with a scuttle and by a woman of about thirty-five next door and by a stair four miles off, still owing the Lowcross bill at nineteen pounds three and fourpence which is named in none of these ten chapters, and still not thanked for anything, and his wife is still at the foot of four steps and has still not asked him how the week went. **He still does not ask the eighth question and he still says why out loud in the room; what the repair changed is the count of how many times he has been there to say it, and the count is now the one the page supports.**

- **Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, book-keeper of the Slade Cut Company, who has kept the books since the second month of next year — the man who holds the half sentence, restored to his own row's fact after Chapter 0331 gave it away.** He wants the rest of it and has wanted it since the third month of the year after and has not asked and is not going to, and the day he asks is the day she stops saying the half she has got, and in Chapter 0333 he worked out that asking would make him a document about one woman, and neither of them is going to. **He was not written into Chapter 0331's point of view and is not now; the sentence names him as the man who keeps the books, which is how Chapter 0332 and Chapter 0338 and the volume outline all name him, and the page is not required to name a man in a chapter that is not his.** His own facts are untouched: the column ruled with no heading over it is still empty, the piece of waste paper is still at the bottom of a box, nineteen days of a crew's money is still called *held* in a weigh-house ledger about half a mile off and is still not in the same column as his own wages, the mark in chalk is still a mark, and nobody asked him anything in this batch and nobody thanked him.

- **The rest of this batch's people, and the repair changed nothing about any of them.** The wife at the foot of four steps: her chapter's hinge was repaired because two consecutive sentences did not parse — *he is about to do a third thing* followed by *the six weeks are made of three things* — and the three items listed are three **places**, not three things to be done, and the *six weeks* belonged to the once he has already told her about. **The sentence now says that what is between this and it is not made of anything she could ask about, and the three are introduced as the three places it goes through, and the not asking on her side is unchanged and is still the same not asking and not a different one. The decision about whether she trusts his judgment is still hers, still unmade, still not on his side of the room, and the repair did not make it and no romance begins and none is implied.** The clerk of about fifty-five: her Chapter 0336 lost the words *in this volume* from one sentence and nothing else, and the third of the three absences she says out loud to a page is unchanged, and she still is not told that he is not coming back. **The man of sixty-one: unchanged, and he walked zero times, and Chapter 0340's sentence about him now says that only one of the two men in that room has walked those nine miles this season, which is true, where the sentence it replaced said *the two thirds are not one third*, which was not, because one of the two thirds was never a third.** Nell Kest: unchanged, two chapters, three women untold and a wage held and a notice in a coat. The two of seventeen: unchanged and unspoken to. The four who cannot read a paragraph: unchanged and unasked, and the man of about fifty-five with a chain is still not one of them. Marn Ottery, Tamsin Rook, Marek Kest: not in this batch's ten chapters and not sent for. **New named figures added by this repair: none, and that is eighteen consecutive batches.**

## VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005, CHAPTERS 341 TO 350 — THE CHARACTER STATE AT THE FIFTH MOVEMENT, AND EVERY FIGURE IN IT RE-MEASURED FROM THE FILES

- **Halla Wray, fifty-one, foreman of the Slade Cut, and the whole of what this batch did to her is that a man walked to a gate and she did not ask.** She is in a box since the first week of the seventh month of the year after, entered by a class, never asked, and in Chapter 0341 nobody asked her about it and nobody is going to. **She still owes the second half of a sentence to the man who keeps the books four hundred yards up the same lane, and he still has not asked, and the day he asks is the day she stops saying the half she has got.** About nine men know and have said nothing for about four years. The man of about fifty-five with a chain went down the lane to a shut gate at about the fifth hour and came back and said nothing about it, and she did not ask him and did not thank him, and that is the whole of what the finding of this volume looked like when it was used once by a person. The two of seventeen were at the back of her bay, unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for, and not going to start. She is not rescued and she is not thanked and no form anywhere was asked to be void.

- **Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, book-keeper of the Slade Cut Company, and the only thing he has wanted for about four years he still has not asked for.** He has kept the books since the second month of next year and that phrase is unchanged. In Chapter 0342 a man of about thirty from the second gang says a true thing out loud in his doorway and he could have put one question there and did not, and the reason is on the page: a question would make the man a party. The piece of waste paper with the half sentence on it is still at the bottom of a box that is not the Company's and he has still not torn it up. His column is still ruled and empty and still has no heading over it. **About nineteen days of a crew's money are still held in a weigh-house ledger about half a mile off under the same word as the Lowcross bill's line, and the two of them were named in the same paragraph in Chapter 0342 and were not put in one column, and the figure of that money is not printed in this batch and is referred to as a thing.** **The fourth thing on the fee board about four hundred yards away is not his, and the about four hundred blanks are not his, and the card file checked every debt in the batch against its holder before a chapter was opened because the last batch gave one debt to two men.** The mark in the chalk on the end wall of the bay is not his and he never went and looked at it.

- **The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, and this is his volume and the last of it.** He is thirty-four or thereabouts, and the work is the copying out of other men's figures until the totals agree, and four days a season he stands on somebody's list and in this batch he stood on none. **He came nine miles in Chapter 0346 for the second time and not the third, on his own two feet, on a day he is not paid for, and the nine miles is on the page for the first time in seven volumes.** He had the asking ready and did not ask it. He said the reason out loud, standing, in about nine sentences, to the man of sixty-one and to a clerk of about fifty-five and to about four other people, and it was the finding and not a speech, and the man of sixty-one did not answer him and was not asked and was not thanked. **The room does not have it afterwards and he is not going to tell anybody what was said in it, and the sixth weeks is not re-derived and the not telling his wife is the same absence as the not asking.** He has a number in his head that he cannot give away and has not given away, and the reason is that the giving of it would be a list and a list of persons is a company, and the last image of the volume is his hand flat on a table with nothing on it and a lamp that is not lit. He is owed the bill, the thing nobody thanked him for, and a wife at the foot of four steps who is not thanked either, and none of the three was collected. **No romance begins and none is implied and the decision about whether anybody trusts anybody's judgment is not on the page and is not made here.**

- **The man of about sixty-one who signs at the foot of every sheet, and the batch put him in a room and did not let anything happen to him.** He is sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two, about nine hundred sheets a year, cannot read a paragraph, and about nine of them a year are not what the person above him wrote and he signed them. **In Chapter 0346 he is on the wrong side of the rail and he does not answer, and he is not asked, and he is not thanked, and the chapter does not date his coming and does not count his visits, so the canon figure of three in nineteen years is neither raised nor lowered on the page.** He wanted about four minutes and about nine people were in that room the second time in nineteen years. **Open item twenty-six is a standing rule for Volume 08: a canon figure that counts visits or turns or non-uses is not a figure a chapter may confirm or deny.**

- **The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in one room, and this batch is the batch in which she said out loud a thing she cannot check.** She writes the minutes, advises nobody, asks nothing, has a window about two feet wide with a pane out of it, and a shelf of eleven books with about four thousand entries in them and no name in any one. **She wrote none of the nine minutes and nobody is going to ask her why she wrote none of it, and that is the whole of her part in the last three chapters of this volume.** Her own question is on the shelf under the window and is still a question and carries no period, and her own name is at the foot of it, and nobody has asked her a second time. **In the ninth month she said at her own table that the man of thirty-four is not coming back, and in Chapter 0347 nobody is going to tell her that he was in that room four days ago, and there is nothing in this empire that would let a clerk of an office be told that a person is coming or that a person is not, and that is the price of standing ready and she has never been paid for it.**

- **Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, two years at the fourth counter, and her chapter is a day on which nothing at all happened and she entered it correctly.** The undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, unretired, unamended and unexercised for about two years, and a correct non-use is entered as one. **The inherited phrase is used once and in its own words, and no date is computed from it: *a person who is not asked has not been given a duty*, and she has known that since about the seventh month of next year.** Nobody came up that stair wanting anything, and she is not told which of the two that is going to be, and the fourth thing in her drawer is untouched, unsent and unopened, with three others. **She has worked out that the line she put in her own book about the woman of thirty-four is a window and not a wall, and the decision about whether she trusts a man's judgment is unmade and is not on the page and this volume does not make it.**

- **Nell Kest, twenty, and the new fact about her in this batch is a decision she made and did not make.** She has told one woman of four that she is not paid and has said the whole of a rule to a second in about four minutes, and the two she cannot reach are still two she cannot reach. **She is not going to ask the man who holds her wage what it is held against, and the reason is the volume's own, and this is hers and it is new and nobody asked her for it.** Her wage has been held since the second week of the third month of the year after. The sixteenth book has her own name at the head of it and has never had a question against it and is not given to anybody and nobody has offered her a key. The notice in a coat is not sent. **She is not going up this river this season and has not said she is never going to, and she is the only person who knows the difference between those two sentences. The woman about nine miles off a building off the old river road is a different person and is not in her chapter and the two of them are kept apart as they have been for seven volumes.**

- **Marn Ottery, thirty-four, eleven years at the other end of the same counter, and she is on the page once in this batch and is not thanked.** She put a coin back on a counter rather than taking it. **The box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks is hers and it is under that counter and it is not resolved, and the fourth thing on the fee board that three clerks cannot account for is hers and the two other clerks' and is not the book-keeper's.** She said the whole of what a blank is out loud to a person twice in eleven years and was not thanked either time, and in this batch nobody asks her about the box and nobody has ever asked her to account for it.

- **The woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage, and she is on the page once in this batch and it is the once that was available to her.** About four years in that building, not in this matter and never in it, and she counts racks in her head without touching the rack. **Her figure has gone up and she has not said by how much and is not going to, and it is not handed on and does not get a heading, and she is not coming up a stair in this season.** The new fact of the batch is structural and not about her: **the rail room at the far end of that passage is not on the passage's work, nothing comes down the flags into it and nothing comes out of it up them, and that is the whole reason what is said in there stays in there.** She has three questions for a man of about sixty-one and has asked none of them, and the fact that he came at all gave her no fourth, and she is not going to be told whether he is coming again. She is not a way out of the volume and Chapter 0347 does not make her one.

- **The man of about fifty-five with a chain, and the batch used the one thing he has had for about four years.** He is not one of the four men who cannot make sense of a paragraph and he says so, and the batch says so, and writing him down as one of them would be a way for a list of four to go wrong. **He went down the lane to a shut gate at about the fifth hour of the fourth day of the third week of the tenth month and came back and said nothing, and nobody asked him and he was not thanked, and the whole of what it bought him is a foreman not asking a question in a bay about nine foot by eleven.**

- **The girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen, and they are in Chapter 0341 and in no other chapter of this batch, and nothing happened to them.** They are at the back of a bay on a written engagement with a rate at the foot of it and one of the four lines of it is the name of a person who has never been in that lane. **Neither was spoken to, neither was thanked, neither was sent for, and neither is going to start, and the girl is not given the chance to say no, and the reader is paid forty-five pence a day and four days a week and what a day comes to is anywhere between fourpence and a shilling according to what gets set on the bench.** No chapter of this batch thanks either of them and no chapter explains their silence as a cost they are bearing.

- **Marek Kest, thirty-eight, and he is not in this batch and the not being in it is entered as a correct non-use.** He is not at a stair, nobody sent him, and he did not go. **He does not exercise the second thing, does not attempt the reading, and does not reach a stage, and the reason he gives out loud in about nine words is on the page in the volume that has just closed and is not paid again in this one.** The mark is a symptom and not a power and does not change. He is not told that a man of thirty-four went nine miles to a room, and the two of them are about four hundred and thirty miles apart and have never met.

- **The others, and none of them was spoken to and none of them was thanked.** A man of about thirty from the second gang, findable, who said a true thing out loud in a doorway and was not asked one word back. A man who came to have a length of wall measured in Chapter 0341 and was gone inside a minute. A carrier paid ninepence at a lock who put four on a shelf under a window with a pane out of it and asked nobody what she carries in about six years. A woman of about forty with a scuttle who said nothing at any point in nine minutes and said nothing after them. A woman of about thirty-five next door who came out on her own step twice. A woman of about thirty-five with a day-book in a lodging house who came about nine miles twice, was not let in and wrote none of it down. A boy of about nine with two slates, the second one with an office question on it and no name on it. About nineteen people behind a bank. About sixty children under sixteen inside a guarantee that is not printed in its own words. Nobody is owed anything by this batch and nobody is paid.

## WHAT THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005 DID TO EACH PRINCIPAL, AT CHAPTER 350, AND IT IS THREE FACTS AND ONE SEPARATION

**Appended by the phase that reviewed Batch 0005 and repaired it. Nothing above this section is rewritten and no consolidated roster above is replaced by a second one. This section records only what the repair changed about a person, and the three things it changed are a location, a number of sentences, and a distance in days. **

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand — one figure moved and nothing about him did.** He is thirty-four or thereabouts, the work is the copying out of other men's figures until the totals agree, four days a season he stands on somebody's list, and in this batch he stood on none. **He came nine miles in Chapter 0346 for the second time and not the third, on his own two feet, on a day he is not paid for, and the room does not have what was said in it and he is not going to tell anybody.** The repair changed one word's worth of arithmetic in Chapter 0346: the reason-speech he says standing up, to the man of sixty-one and to a clerk of about fifty-five and to about four other people, is **in about thirteen sentences and not about nine**, because the speech on the page is nine quoted paragraphs carrying thirteen sentences and this is the chapter where a reader is asked to count. **The nine came from the outline and is still printed in closed Chapter 0340, which this repair may not reopen, so the two figures stand apart on the page and open item 31 carries the divergence.** He is owed the second asking he swore in a doorway he would not do and did, a form for asking a person whether they will mind being told something, the bill for findability, and the thing nobody thanked him for. He has a number in his head that he cannot give it away, and he will not give it to his wife, and the last image of the volume is his hand flat on a table with a lamp in it that is not lit. **No romance begins and none is implied and the decision about whether anybody trusts anybody's judgment is not on the page and was not made here and must not be made in Volume 08's first ten chapters.**

**The clerk of about fifty-five in the office of a seat — one contradiction removed and one number corrected, and her chapter is the better for it.** Nineteen years in one room, about four thousand entries in eleven books and not one name in any of them, which is not carelessness and is the only shape a minute book has got, and a window about two feet wide with a pane out of it that has not been put back in about four years. **The repair removed the one sentence in Volume 07 that said she was in the rail room and wrote none of it, two lines above a paragraph saying she is not told that he was there. She was not in that room. She was in her own room at the top end of the passage at the time of it, and she entered no minute of it, and that is what the chapter always meant, and the beat that follows it is the one the volume is built on: she said in the ninth month that he is not coming back, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that would let a clerk of an office be told that a person is coming or that a person is not.** And the nine minutes are **seven days** behind her, not four. **She is not asked a second time by anybody. She is owed the answer to the question in the second of the eleven books, the fact that she is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing and is paid nothing for standing ready, and about four thousand entries with no name in any one of them.**

**Nell Kest, twenty, dye-house worker, sixteen books, a furnace town about four hundred and thirty miles up this river — one repair, and it is the repair that mattered in this batch.** **Chapter 0350 printed her in a box about nine miles off a building off the old river road, which is the clerk of about fifty-five's building, and that merged two people this state layer has kept apart since Volume 03 and moved Nell Kest about four hundred and thirty miles out of her own counting room at the back of a dye end.** The chapter now places her in a box **about four hundred and thirty miles up this river in a furnace town**, and says in the same paragraph, in bold, that the nine miles off a building off the old river road is a man of about sixty-one and is neither of the two women, and that the two of them are about four hundred and thirty miles apart and neither has ever heard of the other one. Chapter 0344's closing movement, which had put her room at a distance from *a building off a road in another town* without saying which, now names the building as the one off the old river road in a town down the river and names the woman in it as one who stands at the end of a cold passage. **The origin is `outline/volume-07.md`, which places a woman of twenty about nine miles off that building and gives her a wage held, and the outline is not this project's to amend; closed Chapter 0301 prints the merged line and is not reopened, so the collision survives in one file and is carried as open item 29.** She is owed three of the four women untold, the sixteenth book nobody has offered her a key to, a wage held since the second week of the third month of the year after by a man who cannot say what it is held against, and a foreman in a coat with a notice in it that is not sent. **She is not going up this river this season and has not said she is never going to, and she is still the only person who knows the difference between those two sentences, and neither of them is a room anybody can find her in.**

**The woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage — one clarification, and she is still not being thanked and still is not coming up the stair.** Chapter 0344 now names her as standing at the end of a cold passage in the building off the old river road, which is what she has always been on the page, and the repair put the nine miles where it belongs, on the lane off the Slade. **The figure in her head has gone up again and she has not said by how much and is not going to, it is not being handed on, and it is not getting a heading, and the room at the far end of that passage is not on the passage's work and there is no list in that building that reaches it.** She is owed the figure and three questions for a man of about sixty-one that she will not ask. Her chapter's closing clause was one of the five the repair wrote again, and what it now says is that the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is the same as it was when the first rack went down this morning.

**The man of about sixty-one who signs at the foot of every sheet — his address was the thing that was being stolen, and it is his own again.** He signs about nine hundred sheets a year out of a room about nine miles off, he cannot read a paragraph, he has stood on the wrong side of that rail three times in nineteen years, and about nine of those sheets a year are not what the person above him wrote. **Chapter 0350 had begun to give the nine miles to somebody else, and the repair puts it back to him in bold, in the same paragraph that gives the two women their own four hundred and thirty miles, and nothing else about him moved.** He is not one of the four men in a bay and is never in a list with them. He is owed about four minutes, about nine sheets a year, and the reason nobody has asked, which is that he would tell the truth.

**The foreman of fifty-one at the top of the lane off the Slade, the book-keeper of thirty-eight who keeps the books, the woman of thirty-four at the other end of the fourth counter, the man of about thirty from the second gang, the man of about fifty-five with the chain, the girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen, and Marek Kest — none of them is in this section, because the repair changed nothing about any of them, and that is the finding and not an omission.** Halla Wray is still in a box entered by a class and has still never been asked. Rennick Adley's column still has no heading over it and the man of about thirty still got no question back. Tamsin Rook's undertaking is still live, unretired, unamended, unexercised for about two years, and the decision about whether she trusts a man's judgment is still unmade and is still not on the page. The man of about thirty from the second gang still said a true thing in a doorway and was still not thanked. The man with the chain still is not one of the four. The two of seventeen are still unspoken to, unthanked and unsent for. Marek Kest is still not in the room, is not sent for, did not go, and did not exercise the second thing, and the reason he gives out loud is still in about nine words on the page and was not paid a second time. **New named figures added by this repair: none, and the count of consecutive batches that have added none is unchanged at twenty-one, because a repair is not a batch and does not move a figure that counts batches.**

## THE CLOSE OF VOLUME 07, AT CHAPTER 350 — THE PEOPLE AS THEY STAND AT THE CLOSE, IN THE SAME FORM AS THE FIFTH MOVEMENT'S SECTION AND WITH EVERY FIGURE IN IT MEASURED BY THE CLOSE

*Appended downward by the run that took `workspace/volume-07/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, and nothing above this section is rewritten and no consolidated roster above is replaced by a second one. **A close states who is standing where and what is still owed to each of them, and it changes nobody. This section opened no chapter, wrote no prose, and repaired nothing, and no person on this page is different at Chapter 350 from what the page already said at Chapter 349 except that the volume is over.** The next section below is the Volume 08 section and it hands the same people forward with the next-volume question attached.*

- **Halla Wray, fifty-one, foreman of the Slade Cut, and the whole of what this volume did to her is that nobody asked her about a box she has been in since the first week of the seventh month of the year after.** She is in it, entered by a class, never asked, and she says out loud that being in a box and not being asked about it are the same arrangement and that the arrangement was made in a shed by nine families and not by her. **She still owes the second half of a sentence to the man who keeps the books four hundred yards up the same lane, and he still has not asked, and the day he asks is the day she stops saying the half she has got.** About nine men know and have said nothing for about four years. The man of about fifty-five with a chain used the one thing he had, once, at a shut gate, and came back and said nothing, and she did not ask him and did not thank him. The two of seventeen were at the back of her bay, unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for, and not going to start. **She is not rescued, she is not thanked, and no form anywhere has been asked to be void.**

- **Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, book-keeper of the Slade Cut Company, and the only thing he has wanted for about four years he still has not asked for.** He has kept the books since the second month of next year and that phrase is unchanged and no elapsed period is computed from it. A man of about thirty from the second gang said a true thing out loud in his doorway and Adley could have put one question there and did not, and the reason is on the page: a question would make the man a party. **The piece of waste paper with the half sentence on it is still at the bottom of a box that is not the Company's and he has still not torn it up. His column is still ruled and empty and still has no heading over it, and a column with no heading over it is a person, and that is the volume's finding and he is in it.** About nineteen days of a crew's money are still held in a weigh-house ledger about half a mile off. The mark in chalk on the end wall of the bay is not his and he never went and looked at it. **He is owed the second sentence, and the day he asks is the day she stops, and nobody asks in this volume.**

- **The man of about thirty-four with a fair hand, and this was his volume and it is over.** He is thirty-four or thereabouts, the work is the copying out of other men's figures until the totals agree, and four days a season he stands on somebody's list and in the fifth movement he stood on none. **He came about nine miles in Chapter 0346 for the second time and not the third, on his own two feet, on a day he is not paid for, and the nine miles is on the page for the first time in seven volumes.** He had the asking ready and did not ask it, and he said the reason out loud, standing, in about thirteen sentences, to a man of sixty-one and to a clerk of about fifty-five and to about four other people, and it was the finding and not a speech. **The room does not have it afterwards and he is not going to tell anybody what was said in it, and the not telling his wife is the same absence as the not asking.** He has a number in his head that he cannot give away and has not given away, because the giving of it would be a list and a list of persons is a company. The last image of the volume is his hand flat on a table with nothing on it and a lamp that is not lit. He is owed the bill for findability, the thing nobody thanked him for, a form for asking a person whether they will mind being told something, and a wife at the foot of four steps who is not thanked either, and none of the four was collected. **No romance begins and none is implied, and the decision about whether anybody trusts anybody's judgment is unmade, is not on the page, was not made in Volume 07 and may not be made in Volume 08's first ten chapters.** **What he is owed at the close, from Volume 08, is the next asking, and it is nobody's yet, and he is the only person in this matter anybody could put a question to and he is the one nobody has asked.**

- **The man of about sixty-one who signs at the foot of every sheet, and his address is his own again.** He is sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two, about nine hundred sheets a year, cannot read a paragraph, and about nine of them a year are not what the person above him wrote and he signed them and he is the only person alive who knows which nine. **The page reconciles his two figures itself: he has said the whole of it out loud twice in nineteen years, in that room on two of his three visits, and the third visit is a month that is gone and is not dated on the page and is not going to be.** In Chapter 0346 he is on the wrong side of the rail and he does not answer, and he is not asked, and he is not thanked. **He wants about four minutes to say no to about half of those sheets and yes to the rest, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a man to put his mark beside a sum and call it a disagreement, and he has never been asked anything by anybody at all.** He is not one of the four men in a bay and never is, and he is owed the asking and the four minutes and the reason nobody has asked, which is that he would tell the truth.

- **The clerk of about fifty-five, nineteen years in one room, and she said out loud a thing in the ninth month that she cannot check.** She writes the minutes, advises nobody, asks nothing, and has a window about two feet wide with a pane out of it and a shelf of eleven books with about four thousand entries in them and no name in any one. **She wrote none of the nine minutes and nobody is going to ask her why she wrote none of it, and that is the whole of her part in the last movement.** Her own question is on the shelf under the window and is still a question, carries no period, and has her own name at the foot of it, and nobody has asked her a second time. **She is the only person in this matter anybody could serve a question on in writing and she is paid nothing.** In the ninth month she said at her own table that the man of thirty-four is not coming back, **and no chapter of this volume told her whether he was in that room, and there is nothing in this empire that would let a clerk of an office be told that a person is coming or that a person is not, and that is the price of standing ready and she has never been paid for it.**

- **Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, two years at the fourth counter, and her volume was a day on which nothing at all happened and she entered it correctly.** The undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, unretired, unamended and unexercised for about two years, and a correct non-use is entered as one. **The inherited phrase is used once and in its own words and no date is computed from it: a person who is not asked has not been given a duty, and she has known that since about the seventh month of next year.** Nobody came up that stair wanting anything, and she is not told which of the two things that is going to be, and the fourth thing in her drawer is untouched, unsent and unopened, with three others. She has worked out that the line she put in her own book about the woman at the other end of the same counter is a window and not a wall. **The decision about whether she trusts a man's judgment is unmade and is not on the page, and Volume 07 did not make it and Volume 08's first ten chapters may not.**

- **Nell Kest, twenty, dye-house worker, sixteen books, a furnace town about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, and the repair that mattered in this volume's last batch was the removal of a merge.** She is in a box entered in the fifth month of the year after and the wage behind it has been held since the second week of the third month of the year after by a man who cannot say what it is held against, **and she is not going to ask him what it is held against, and that decision is hers and is new and nobody asked her for it.** She has told one woman of four that she is not paid and has said the whole of a rule to a second in about four minutes, and the two she cannot reach are still two she cannot reach. The sixteenth book has her own name at the head of it and has never had a question against it and is not given to anybody and nobody has offered her a key. The notice in a coat is not sent. **She is not going up this river this season and has not said she is never going to, and she is the only person who knows the difference between those two sentences, and neither of them is a room anybody can put her in.** She is owed three of the four women untold, the sixteenth book, the wage, and the foreman's notice. **The woman about nine miles off a building off the old river road is a different person, and the two of them are about four hundred and thirty miles apart and neither has ever heard of the other one, and Chapter 0301 of Volume 07 still prints the merge and is not reopened.**

- **Marn Ottery, thirty-four, eleven years at the other end of the same counter, and she is on the page in this volume and is not thanked.** She put a coin back on a counter rather than taking it. **The box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks is hers and it is under that counter and it is not resolved, and the fourth thing on the fee board that three clerks cannot account for is hers and the two other clerks' and is not the book-keeper's.** She said the whole of what a blank is out loud to a person twice in eleven years and was not thanked either time, and nobody in this volume has ever asked her to account for the box. **She is owed the whole of what a blank is, said twice, to nobody.**

- **The woman of about twenty-six at the end of the cold passage, and she is on the page and she is not being thanked and she is not coming up the stair.** About four years in that building, not in this matter and never in it, and she counts racks in her head without touching the rack. **Her figure has gone up and she has not said by how much and is not going to, it is not being handed on, and it is not getting a heading, and the room at the far end of that passage is not on the passage's work, which is the whole reason what is said in there stays in there.** She has three questions for a man of about sixty-one and has asked none of them, and the fact that he came at all gave her no fourth, and she is not going to be told whether he is coming again. **She is owed the figure and three questions she will not ask, and the volume closed without either moving.**

- **The man of about fifty-five with a chain, and the volume used the one thing he has had for about four years, once.** He is not one of the four men who cannot make sense of a paragraph and he says so, and writing him down as one of them would be a way for a list of four to go wrong. **He went down the lane to a shut gate at about the fifth hour and came back and said nothing, and nobody asked him and he was not thanked, and the whole of what it bought him is a foreman not asking a question in a bay about nine foot by eleven.** He is owed the fact that he used it and that nobody knows.

- **The girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen, and they are in this volume and nothing happened to them.** They are at the back of a bay on a written engagement with a rate at the foot of it and one of the four lines of it is the name of a person who has never been in that lane. **Neither was spoken to, neither was thanked, neither was sent for, and neither is going to start, and the girl has never been given the chance to say no.** The reader is paid forty-five pence a day and four days a week and what a day comes to is anywhere between four pence and a shilling according to what gets set on the bench. **No chapter of fifty thanks either of them and no chapter explains their silence as a cost they are bearing.**

- **Marek Kest, thirty-eight, and he is not in this volume's last movement and the not being in it is entered as a correct non-use.** He is not at a stair, nobody sent him, and he did not go. **He does not exercise the second thing, does not attempt the reading, and does not reach a stage, and the reason he gives out loud in about nine words is on the page in the volume that has just closed and may not be paid a second time: there is no form in this empire for compelling a person to be brought to a room.** The mark is a symptom and not a power and does not change. He is not told that a man of thirty-four went nine miles to a room, and the two of them are about four hundred and thirty miles apart and have never met. **He is owed the labour he chose out of his own hand, which is not re-decided, and he is not a stage and not a certification and not a party to anything this volume produced.**

- **The others, and none of them was spoken to and none of them was thanked.** A man of about thirty from the second gang, findable, who said a true thing out loud in a doorway and was not asked one word back. A man who came to have a length of wall measured and was gone inside a minute. A carrier paid ninepence at a lock who put four on a shelf under a window with a pane out of it and asked nobody what she carries in about six years. A woman of about forty with a scuttle who said nothing at any point in nine minutes and said nothing after them. A woman of about thirty-five next door who came out on her own step twice. A woman of about thirty-five with a day-book in a lodging house who came about nine miles twice, was not let in and wrote none of it down. A boy of about nine with two slates, the second one with an office question on it and no name on it. About nineteen people behind a bank. About sixty children under sixteen inside a guarantee that is not printed in its own words. **Nobody is owed anything by this volume and nobody is paid, and the four hundred and thirty miles is walked zero times and the nine miles is walked twice.**

Payoff target: a repair phase, which this project does not currently schedule. |
| **The woman of about twenty-six, and the figure she will not give** | **OPEN, UNCHANGED, AND ONE OF HER FIGURES IS GONE FROM THE SENTENCE AROUND IT.** She is not going to give it a heading and is not going to hand it on, she has three questions and will ask none of them, and she is not coming up a stair. **The repair removed a period of about nineteen weeks that sat four lines below the date that makes it impossible, and the sentence now says the figure has gone up in every year since the seventh month of the year after, which is what her own date supports and computes nothing.** She is not a device and the chapter does not say what she is. |
| **The man of about sixty-one, and the address** | **OPEN, UNCHANGED, AND HIS CHAPTER LOST AN INVENTED FIGURE.** Chapter 0307 had said about four hundred sheets a year going into about four hundred other rooms and one sheet in five going nowhere near the room, and the canon is about nine hundred sheets a year and the four hundred belongs to a box of blanks under a counter in another chapter. **Both are gone and the line says about nine hundred sheets a year go out of that room and not one of them comes back into it.** He is still not a document, a list, a route or a symbol, and he is still the one thing on a sheet that can be asked anything at all. |
| **The two people with a wage held, and the collision that is on the record since Batch 0001 closed** | **OPEN, AND IT IS STILL A REPAIR ITEM, AND THE REPAIR DID NOT ADD TO IT.** The woman about nine miles off the building off the old river road and Nell Kest in the counting room four hundred and thirty miles up this river are both described in this layer with a wage held, and they are two people the page has kept apart on purpose. **Chapter 0302 still does not mention the other woman at all, which is the correct handling, and a Volume 07 writer who brings them together merges two people the page has separated.** Payoff target: a state-layer consolidation, which is not a writer's decision. |
| **The fifteen-word window, and the fact that it is still not in the standing check list** | **OPEN, AND IT IS NOW THE ONLY ONE OF THE THREE SWEEPS THAT FOUND ANYTHING IN THE BATCH THAT FOLLOWED THE REPAIR TOO.** The repair changed Chapter 0303's opening and the window's readings moved in both directions depending on the unit, which is the standing lesson arriving unpredicted. **On the card file's unit the figure rose from 86 to 97 at fifteen and from 117 to 129 at thirteen; on a per-pair sum it fell from 498 to 426, and the per-file figures fell in all ten files.** Both are printed with their units named, and adding the window to the standing list is the cheapest thing available to whoever maintains that list and is still not a writer's job. |
| **Three shapes no check in this project can see, added to the standing list by this repair** | **OPEN, AND ALL THREE ARE CHEAP.** A run-of-emphasis check that counts the sentences inside a run and not only the markers, because a parity test cannot see a run holding two sentences and Chapter 0306 held one. A blank-line-collapse check, because three blank lines inside a paragraph is a shape this house style produces and Chapter 0308 had three and no named test covered it. And a calendar check that re-adds the step from each chapter to the next and compares it with any interval the narration states, because two of the three dates the review found were of this kind and not one sweep in this project can see them. |
| **The four debts, and the corrected number, and what a review is, and this repair claims none** | **OPEN, ALL FOUR, AND UNCHANGED, AND THE GATE HAS NOW OPENED ONCE.** An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which opened for the first time on Volume 07 Batch 0001 and returned twenty-two findings. The six open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, and a seventh that Batch 0001 added. **A repair is not a review and a second pass by the writing hand is not a second reading, and this run applied findings written by somebody else, and that is a third act and discharges nothing.** |

## VOLUME 07, BATCH 0002 — THE THREAD LEDGER AT CHAPTER 320, every row in the present tense, every row carrying a payoff target or an explicit em dash

*Appended by the run that took `workspace/volume-07/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, after its last edit. Nothing above this point is rewritten. **A self-audit and not a review, and it discharges none of the four debts. The standing lesson is carried: a bound is measured after the last edit with the method stated; a sweep that has not been run is not a result; a test that returns zero is evidence of nothing until you have checked what shape of damage it is blind to; a check that is blind is written down as blind in the same record that claims it clean.** Every row below is a fact a later writer may rely on, and every row is stated in the present tense because a thread in the past tense is a thread that has been dropped.*

1. **The volume's shape-question, and it is not the who.** How is a thing asked in about nine hundred buildings at once by people who cannot be instructed to ask it. **The second movement found the edge of it and the edge is structural: the only thing this empire has ever been able to put in front of about nine hundred rooms at once is a class, and a class is a heading, and a heading is a company.** Payoff target: Chapters 0341 to 0350, the fifth movement, where the answer is given in the negative in a room and not in a book.
2. **The who, and it is Volume 06's, and it is still unanswered, and unanswered is not the same as closed.** No chapter of this batch names anybody, and no chapter of this batch moves it. Payoff target: not this volume, and the outline does not restate it in any form a writer could put in a mouth.
3. **The count of askings, and it is seven, and it did not move in Batch 0001 and it did not move here.** Six question marks are printed in these ten files, every one of them in a mouth and every one of them about a sheet, a day, a figure, a letter, an entry or a light, and not one of them put to a person about a person. Payoff target: Chapter 0346 to 0348, where the man of thirty-four has the eighth question ready and does not ask it.
4. **The address: a man of about sixty-one who signs at the foot of about nine hundred sheets a year.** He is the second hand since he was forty-two, he cannot read a paragraph, about nine of about nine hundred a year have not been what the person above them wrote, he signed those, and he cannot write one of them down. **In Chapter 0320 the finding reaches him as an ordinary morning and not as a revelation, and it is a finding he has been living in for about nineteen years without having the words for it.** Payoff target: Chapter 0323, the second day of the first week of the sixth month, which is the season and is the third movement's whole hinge.
5. **The movement's finding, and it is now on the page in four places and is load-bearing for the rest of the volume: a column with a heading over it is a machine, and a column with no heading over it is a person.** A blank is the only paper in this empire that cannot be held against anybody. A book-keeper's column with no names in it and no heading over it. A column headed *next review* with nothing in it in about nine years. A sixth ruled space with no heading over it that about two hundred and forty people have never known is there. Payoff target: the fifth movement, where the answer is that a person is the one thing a class cannot be asked about.
6. **The one form that ever went to a scale: a House's border law, about two hundred years old, requiring that a thing offered to a border household at no charge be answered, that the answer be entered, and that the entering of it be a form and not a letter.** It got an answer in about three weeks and the households refused it and the refusals are entered. **No House, no seat and no notary is named anywhere in these ten chapters and the House's name is on the closed page and is deliberately not used.** Payoff target: the fifth movement, where the finding is that the whole of the machinery of asking at a scale is the machinery of making people into companies.
7. **The border form is not one of the seven, and Chapter 0311 says why out loud.** No person was in it, nobody put it to anybody, and a class is the only thing a form can be put in front of without somebody in a room becoming a party. This is what keeps the count safe and it is the closest anything in this matter has come to an asking. Payoff target: — held, and the reason it is held is that it is not an asking.
8. **Rennick Adley's waste paper.** He wrote the finding out in a fair hand on a piece of waste paper about four months after he had it, and the waste paper is at the bottom of a box in his counting room, and nobody has seen it, and he is not going to show it to anybody. **The finding on that paper is the finding in his words and is not the finding in a clerk's mouth, and the two are the same fact arrived at in two rooms that do not know about each other.** Payoff target: the fifth movement, or never, and nobody is going to ask him for it.
9. **The second sentence a foreman of fifty-one owes a book-keeper four hundred yards up that lane, and has owed since the third month of the year after.** He wants the rest of it, has wanted it since, has not asked and is not going to, and the day he asks is the day she stops saying the half she has got. **In Chapter 0315 he did not cross the lane to where she was standing and he did not go into the bay.** Payoff target: — nobody asks in this volume, and the outline says so.
10. **The finding that a person is the one thing a class cannot be asked about, owed to a foreman of fifty-one since the seventh month of the year after.** She has it in a room and not in a book. Payoff target: the fourth movement, where it is stated in a mouth and not in a book.
11. **The question in the second of the eleven books, and it is still a question.** It went in on the fourth day of the third week of the ninth month of the year after next, on her own motion, at nobody's request, with the day and the hour on it and her own name at the foot of it, and it is on a shelf under a window with a pane out of it. **No elapsed period is stated for it anywhere in this batch and none may be, because `outline/volume-07.md` and closed Chapters 0260 and 0295 are a year apart, and that conflict is a repair item and not a resolution.** Payoff target: — it stays a question and does not become an answer and does not become an order, and nobody asks its author a second time.
12. **The figure at the end of the cold passage.** A woman of about twenty-six has counted the racks that come back light by one in her head for about four years, the figure has gone up in every year since the seventh month of the year after, and she is not giving it and is not going to. **In Chapter 0317 she found the sixth ruled space at the bottom of somebody else's page and worked out in about four minutes that it is not a person either, because nobody has ever been introduced to it.** Payoff target: — she is not coming up a stair in this volume, and Movement 5 is not this one.
13. **The three questions the woman of about twenty-six has for the man of about sixty-one.** None asked, and none is going to be, and there is no form in this empire that would let anybody in that building ask her where they are. Payoff target: —.
14. **The four men in a bay who cannot read a paragraph, unnamed, and the man of about fifty-five with a chain, who is not one of them and says so.** None of the four was asked one question about a number in this batch, and the man with the chain was not asked one either. **In Chapter 0315 he was in the bay in the morning and said nothing, and putting him down as one of the four would be a way for a list of four to go wrong.** Payoff target: — the four are not a method and are not going to be used by anybody in this volume.
15. **The girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen at the back of that bay on a written engagement.** Neither has been spoken to, thanked or sent for in this batch and neither is going to start. **In Chapter 0315 the book-keeper went up the lane past the door without going in and without stopping, and that is the whole of what he did about them.** Payoff target: Chapters 0331 to 0340, the fourth movement, and a chapter that thanks either of them breaks this volume.
16. **The woman of twenty about nine miles off a building off the old river road, in a box, whose wage has been held, and the woman of twenty in a counting room about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, whose wage has also been held.** **They are two people and they are kept apart on purpose and Chapter 0302 does not mention the other one at all, and no chapter of this batch brings them together.** The collision in the state layer is still on the record and is still unresolved. Payoff target: —.
17. **The box of about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks under the fourth counter, with no number over it, no day, no name and no heading.** It is the nearest thing to a post anybody has built in this empire and it is not a post, and the only thing that would turn it into a post is a person asking for it, and a person asking for it is told no by a person. **In Chapter 0318 it is the proof of the movement's finding and it is still not a post and nothing was resolved about it.** Payoff target: —.
18. **The fourth thing on the board behind the fourth counter, in a hand that is not one of the hands in that room, and the fourth thing on a fee board about four hundred yards away that a book-keeper cannot account for either.** Neither has been accounted for in this batch and neither is going to be, and there is no form in this empire for accounting for a line of writing on a wall. Payoff target: —.
19. **The two boxes on a page ruled by hand in a clerk's own book with the same name in both, and the line about a clerk of thirty-four saying no on a stair with no reason given.** She is not going to rule a third line, she is not going to give the two boxes a heading, and **in Chapter 0319 she worked out in about four minutes that a heading over them would make a company out of a woman of thirty-four who has been at that counter eleven years and has never been asked about anything.** Payoff target: —.
20. **The undertaking of the fourth of the first month, live, unretired, unamended and unexercised.** **No new ordinal number was given to it in this batch and none may be: the sixth was spent in an earlier month and a batch that reprinted the count would be computing a period out of it.** The sixth reason was that nobody asked her and nobody asked her in this batch either. Payoff target: —, and the sixth time is the last time the count moves and it does not move here.
21. **The decision about whether she trusts a man's judgment, which is unmade, is not on the page, and is not made in this batch and is not gestured at.** No romance begins and none is implied and a chapter that reached for one has left this manuscript. Payoff target: —.
22. **The fourth thing in the drawer under that counter, untouched, unsent and unopened, with three others.** Payoff target: —.
23. **The bill at Lowcross, at nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable, named once in Chapter 0312 and not again.** He did not pay it and he is not the reason it is unpaid. Payoff target: —.
24. **The guarantee offered and unanswered, with about sixty children under sixteen inside it, not printed in its own words and with no child in it named.** Payoff target: —.
25. **About nineteen people still behind about four hundred and forty foot of bank who said no again in the same words, and have not been asked again.** Payoff target: —.
26. **A single return goes to one district out of about nine hundred districts, and about nine hundred sheets a year go out of one building off the old river road, and not one of them comes back into it.** Payoff target: the third movement, which is where the address cannot be asked in the building that made it.
27. **The two instruments that require her in any room he is in, which are not one instrument and are not merged with the two boxes.** **He is not at the top of a stair in this movement, nobody sent him, and he does not go.** Payoff target: the fourth movement, where the reason is said out loud to about nine men in a bay and she is not told.
28. **The mark in chalk on a wall in the bay at the height of the third link of a chain, which means nothing, is nobody's, is about four years off being anybody's evidence, and is not converted by anything in this batch.** Payoff target: —.
29. **A bar of his own came off Marek Kest on the twelfth of the ninth month of last year on an application of one line which he wrote himself, and he holds no certification, is in no office, no fund and no list, and is not at a stage and is not going to get to one.** **He appears in none of these ten chapters, at no stair, and nothing in this batch exercises anything.** Payoff target: the fourth movement, where the reason he does not attempt the reading is his own and is given out loud.
30. **The four hundred and thirty miles and the nine miles, both walked zero times in this batch.** Payoff target: the third movement walks the nine miles once and nobody sends him.
31. **The card-language and month and weekday sweeps, the emphasis joint, and the fifteen-word window.** All clean on these ten files, and the standing check list still does not carry the fifteen-word window, which was the only sweep in this batch that found anything. Payoff target: — and it is not a writer's job.
32. **FOUND BY THIS PHASE AND NOT REPAIRED: Chapter 0303 carries a fifth year name with one *after the year* too many in it, which makes it the sixth year.** It should read *the year after the year after the year after next*, as its own card, its own chapter summary, the character state and the volume outline all read it. **The file has been left standing and this phase names itself as the phase that found it, and no sweep in this project could have found it, because none of them knows what the right date is.** Payoff target: a repair phase on Volume 07 Batch 0001, which this project does not currently schedule.
33. **The two repairs this batch did make on its own ten chapters, both of them found by a test and not by reading: a bold run opened inside a speech turn and closed paragraphs later, in five paragraphs across four files, which the per-paragraph parity test could not see because the run is even at the file level; and three paragraphs exactly duplicated between Chapter 0313 and Chapter 0319, which the duplicated-paragraph test and the forty-word window both saw.** Payoff target: — closed, and both are named in the batch summary with the shape of damage beside each.
